==================== START RECORD ====================
[Objective]:
Critic_Check

[Prompt]:
Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-10-28
- Trajectory: ['Rock Club#144 at 8:10', 'Lottery Retailer#41 at 8:00', 'Electronics Store#676 at 17:30', 'Supermarket#1773 at 22:30', 'Multiplex#283 at 22:45']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`
Now, analyze the provided input data and generate your response.

[Result]:
```json
{
  "evaluation": "The sequence of activities is temporally illogical as the 'Rock Club' is visited at 8:10 AM, which is an unusual time for such a venue to be open or for someone to be active there."
}
```
==================== END RECORD ====================

- Date: 2019-10-28
- Trajectory: ['Rock Club#144 at 08:10', 'Lottery Retailer#41 at 08:00', 'Bookstore#14 at 09:00', 'Electronics Store#676 at 17:30', 'Multiplex#283 at 22:00']

[Prompt]:
Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-10-30
- Trajectory: ['Rock Club#144 at 8:10', 'Lottery Retailer#41 at 8:20', 'Sake Bar#696 at 19:00', 'Sushi Restaurant#1237 at 20:30', 'Japanese Restaurant#4274 at 21:45', 'Landmark#612 at 22:15', 'Multiplex#283 at 23:00']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]

[Objective]:

[Prompt]:
Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-10-31
- Trajectory: ['Rock Club#144 at 08:00', 'Lottery Retailer#41 at 08:00', 'Electronics Store#676 at 17:30', 'Pharmacy#133 at 18:30', 'Discount Store#1414 at 19:30', 'Landmark#612 at 20:30', 'Multiplex#283 at 22:00']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`
Now, analyze the provided input data and generate your response.

[Result]:
```json
"Rock clubs are typically not open at 08:00 in the morning."
```

[Objective]:
Critic_Check

[Prompt]:
Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-11-04
- Trajectory: ['Lottery Retailer#41 at 08:00', 'Rock Club#144 at 08:10', 'Electronics Store#676 at 17:30', 'Supermarket#1773 at 22:30', 'Multiplex#283 at 23:00']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`
Now, analyze the provided input data and generate your response.

[Result]:
```json
"Rock Club#144 at 08:10 is highly implausible as rock clubs are typically open in the evening and night, not in the morning."
```
==================== END RECORD ====================

1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?

- Date: 2019-11-12

1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?

you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.

- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.

[Prompt]:
Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-11-18
- Trajectory: ['Lottery Retailer#41 at 08:00', 'Rock Club#144 at 08:10', 'Bookstore#14 at 12:00', 'Electronics Store#676 at 17:30', 'Supermarket#1773 at 22:30', 'Multiplex#283 at 22:45']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`
Now, analyze the provided input data and generate your response.

[Result]:
```json
{
  "evaluation": "Rock clubs are typically not open at 08:10 in the morning."
}
```

you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-11-20
- Trajectory: ['Lottery Retailer#41 at 08:00', 'Electronics Store#676 at 17:30', 'Sake Bar#696 at 19:00', 'Sushi Restaurant#1237 at 20:30', 'Japanese Restaurant#4274 at 22:00', 'Supermarket#1773 at 22:30', 'Multiplex#283 at 22:45', 'Landmark#612 at 23:00']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.

-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
